## DEC-000002 | 2026-04-23 17:53:00 CST

- Topic: First paper-pack venue filter
- Requested by: user
- Decision: The first downloadable literature pack is restricted to top-tier venues only, using `CVPR`, `ICCV`, `ECCV`, `NeurIPS`, and `TPAMI` as the selection boundary. Conference PDFs should come from official proceedings when open access is available; journal papers may use arXiv PDFs when the publisher PDF is not openly accessible, but the official DOI/source page must still be recorded.
- Rationale: This keeps the initial literature base high-quality, reproducible, and easy to migrate, while avoiding workshop papers and low-confidence sources.

This batch is organized independently as `papers/top_venues/batch_02`.
- Rationale: After building a broad first pack, the next useful step for our project is to concentrate on methods that are more directly connected to ultra-high-resolution restoration and scalable inference.

## DEC-000004 | 2026-04-23 18:20:00 CST

## DEC-000005 | 2026-04-23 18:38:00 CST

- Topic: Third paper-pack focus
- Requested by: user
- Decision: The third literature batch should follow the diffusion / generative restoration line while keeping a bridge to real-world degradation modeling. Concretely, it combines foundational papers on real-image degradation and blind SR with more recent top-venue papers on diffusion-based, generative, or multimodal SR/restoration. This batch is organized independently as `papers/top_venues/batch_03`.
- Rationale: For a top-tier ultra-high-resolution restoration project, it is important not only to track the latest generative SR methods, but also to understand the degradation assumptions and data-generation gaps they depend on.

## DEC-000006 | 2026-04-23 21:03:00 CST

- Rationale: This gives us a stable structure for later reading notes, gap analysis, and project idea generation without moving the original PDFs or breaking reproducibility.

## DEC-000007 | 2026-04-23 21:09:00 CST

- Topic: Project direction baseline
- Requested by: user
- Decision: The project is aimed at an `IEEE Transactions on Image Processing (TIP)` submission on `超高清图像复原`. The current high-level direction is to study a texture/frequency-aware 2D Gaussian-guided restoration framework: first downsample the image, then perform improved 2D Gaussian processing in the lower-resolution space with extra texture-related parameters, produce an attention tensor, and finally map that guidance back to the original-resolution image through affine transformation or affine modulation.
- Rationale: This direction keeps the project centered on the core research contradiction in UHD restoration: how to preserve rich high-resolution detail while avoiding the parameter explosion of dense Gaussian modeling directly on the full-resolution image.

## DEC-000008 | 2026-04-23 22:10:00 CST

The official paper line is defined as a cross-resolution, texture/frequency-aware 2D Gaussian-guided framework for UHD image restoration, and future discussion should treat this document as the primary reference rather than temporary brainstorming notes or implementation-heavy drafts.
- Rationale: The project now has enough context to move from loose idea collection to a stable research baseline, which is necessary to keep later method design, experiments, and writing aligned with the intended TIP contribution.

## DEC-000015 | 2026-04-24 09:34:31 CST

- Requested by: user

All training runs under this architecture should use `A**` identifiers, such as `A01` and `A02`. ## DEC-000017 | 2026-04-24 11:16:18 CST

- Topic: Training-template and checkpoint archival policy
- Requested by: user
- Decision: Training under the same architecture version must use explicit training templates instead of rewriting training code from scratch for every run. Training templates should be versioned as reusable code baselines, and every training run must independently preserve the exact code template snapshot it used, its configuration, logs, and all produced checkpoints/weights. The project should favor full archival over space saving, because disk capacity is not currently the limiting factor. Any historical version should be recoverable for resuming training, reproducing results, or later research.
- Rationale: Separating architecture versions, training-template versions, and training-run/checkpoint versions prevents experiment drift and makes all runs traceable even after the active codebase changes.

## DEC-000018 | 2026-04-24 11:20:49 CST

- Topic: Training-duration tiers and deployment-gate policy
- Requested by: user
- Decision: Future training should be organized into three duration tiers: short training no longer than 1 hour, medium training no longer than 24 hours, and long training measured in days. Experiments should normally move from short training for quick validation/falsification, to medium training for stability and long-run preparation, and only then to long training when value is clear. Every training deployment, including fresh starts and resumes, must pass a documented deployment gate before launch. The gate must be checked against `docs/training_deployment_gate.md`, with special attention to correct dataset usage, avoidance of contamination from previous runs in parameters/protocol/checkpoints, and explicit confirmation of batch size and planned training duration.
- Rationale: Tiered training improves time utilization and prevents expensive long runs from being started with wrong data, stale parameters, inherited checkpoints, or unverified runtime settings.

## DEC-000019 | 2026-04-24 11:23:36 CST

- Requested by: user

Writing only to `decisions`, `operations`, or auxiliary docs is not enough; `messages` must preserve the user-facing dialogue and the assistant's confirmation/summary.
- Rationale: The message log is the primary chronological record of the collaboration. Future review should be able to reconstruct not only what files changed, but also what was agreed in conversation.

## DEC-000020 | 2026-04-24 11:35:49 CST

- Topic: First training preparation baseline
- Requested by: user
- Decision: The first prepared training deployment is `V1/T01/A01`. `T01` is the first reusable training template for the `V1` architecture, and `A01` is a short-training run on the accepted paired dataset `UHD-LL_down4`. The run uses patch-based supervised restoration with random `256x256` training crops, batch size `2`, AdamW, Charbonnier loss plus a small gradient loss, validation on a limited `UHD-LL_down4` validation subset, and a 55-minute wall-clock cap. The run-specific gate and manifest are stored under `experiments/V1/runs/A01/`, and the current template/code snapshot has been archived there.
- Rationale: `UHD-LL_down4` is the safest first dataset for validating the training loop because it is paired, accepted, smaller than full UHD, and fast enough for a short training tier. Preparing `A01` through the gate/template/archive system verifies the version-management policy before any longer training investment.

## DEC-000022 | 2026-04-24 20:04:03 CST

- Topic: Validation and test split policy
- Requested by: user
- Decision: Training-time acceptance/validation sets should be fixed subsets carved from the official training split, while official test splits should be reserved for final post-training evaluation. For `V1/T01/A01`, the acceptance/validation set is the first `100` deterministic filename-ordered pairs from `UHD-LL_down4/training_set`, the effective training set is the remaining `1900` pairs, and the final test set is `UHD-LL_down4/testing_set` with `150` pairs. Temporary preflight may use a smaller subset of the internal validation set, but it is not a reportable metric.

## DEC-000023 | 2026-04-25 00:37:09 CST

- Topic: A01 first-deployment smoke calibration
- Requested by: user
- Decision: The first deployment `V1/T01/A01` should use full-image `UHD-LL_down4` training with `batch_size=28`, `train_crop_size=0`, `max_steps=4000`, `wall_clock_minutes=58`, `val_interval=200`, and `checkpoint_interval=200`. Smoke calibration on real batches measured peak CUDA reserved memory of about `89.453G` for batch 28, within the requested `85G-93G` range. Batch 29 also ran but reached about `92.680G`, too close to the upper bound for the first deployment.
- Rationale: Batch 28 satisfies the user's memory target while preserving a safer margin below 93G. The 20-step stable smoke measured about `0.851s` per step, making 4000 steps fit approximately within the requested 1-hour deployment window with a 58-minute wall-clock cap.

## DEC-000027 | 2026-04-25 11:12:18 CST

- Topic: A01 result-analysis conclusion
- Requested by: user
- Decision: `V1/T01/A01` is considered a successful first deployment and positive feasibility signal. It completed `4300` steps in about `59.40` minutes, produced complete checkpoints and metrics, and the final checkpoint achieved `25.7391 dB` PSNR on the official `UHD-LL_down4` test split in post-hoc full evaluation. The final checkpoint also outperformed the step-1800 checkpoint on full internal validation and official test. However, A01's training-time validation used only `10` validation batches, so future runs should use a larger or full internal validation protocol for checkpoint selection.
- Rationale: A01 proves that the V1 model and T01 training template are trainable end-to-end on real paired data with the target memory profile, but the noisy 10-sample validation loop is not strong enough for reliable model selection in later experiments.

## DEC-000031 | 2026-04-25 14:42:00 CST

- Topic: Next experiment strategy after A01
- Requested by: user
- Decision: The recommended next experiment is `V1/T01/A02`, still on `UHD-LL_down4`, not an immediate jump to a new task. A02 should keep the proven A01 backbone, full-image training, and calibrated `batch_size=28`, but fix the two main A01 weaknesses: use full or much larger internal validation for checkpoint selection, and set the step/time budget so the run clearly reaches at least one hour if deployed as a short run. The goal of A02 is to turn A01's positive feasibility signal into a stable checkpoint-selection and training-protocol baseline. After A02 validates this protocol, the next branch should move to a cross-task short run on an accepted UHD dataset such as `UHD_haze` or `UHD_deblur`.
- Rationale: A01 already proved that V1/T01 can train end-to-end and fit the target memory profile, but its 10-batch training-time validation was too noisy and the final runtime was slightly under 60 minutes. Fixing protocol reliability on the same dataset isolates the experimental variable; only after that should cross-task generalization be tested.

## DEC-000032 | 2026-04-25 17:32:21 CST

- Topic: Adjust next-run dataset plan after adding new train sets
- Requested by: user
- Decision: The next experiment plan is adjusted to account for the newly accepted train sets. `V1/T01/A02` should no longer be treated as only a `UHD-LL_down4` replay. The recommended immediate next run is a short, patch-based single-task probe on `UHD_haze`, using a fixed internal validation subset from the train split and reserving the local `231`-pair test split for final evaluation. `V1/T01/A03` should then probe `UHD_deblur` under the same discipline. `UHD-LL_down4` remains useful as a control or fallback replay, but it is no longer the only next-run dataset. `4K` and `8K` should not be used by current `T01` until a super-resolution-capable template exists.

## DEC-000033 | 2026-04-25 18:05:00 CST

- Topic: A02 deployment adjusted to A_a02 after data-loader bottleneck
- Requested by: user
- Decision: The initial `V1/T01/A02` launch on `UHD_haze` was stopped during the startup window after reaching step `30`, because end-to-end training was dominated by 4K JPEG decoding/DataLoader time and would likely not reach the first planned validation/checkpoint before the `58` minute cap. The run is preserved as an aborted deployment record. The active deployment is the derivative `V1/T01/A_a02`, using the same dataset and task but with data-inclusive smoke calibration, `batch_size=16`, `num_workers=8`, `max_steps=2000`, `val_interval=250`, `checkpoint_interval=250`, and `wall_clock_minutes=58`. `A_a02` has passed startup validation: it reached step `400` by about `6.06` minutes, completed the step-250 validation with `100` batches, and wrote `step_000250.pt` plus `latest.pt`.
- Rationale: This preserves version traceability while avoiding a known bad deployment configuration. The user requested deployment, but a deployment that cannot reach validation/checkpoint inside the short-run cap is not useful. `A_a02` keeps the intended UHD-Haze experiment while correcting the runtime bottleneck with measured, data-inclusive settings.

## DEC-000034 | 2026-04-25 18:11:21 CST

- Topic: A_a02 low VRAM occupancy and data-pipeline priority
- Requested by: user
- Decision: The user's concern that `A_a02` uses too little GPU memory is correct. `A_a02` should continue as the active short-run probe because it reaches validation/checkpoint milestones, but it should not be treated as the final efficient UHD-Haze training configuration. The low memory usage is a consequence of choosing `batch_size=16` to work around the current 4K JPEG/DataLoader bottleneck. Future UHD-Haze runs should prioritize data-pipeline optimization, such as a pre-cropped patch cache or a more efficient storage/loader format, then recalibrate batch size to recover high GPU utilization.
- Rationale: High VRAM occupancy by itself is not useful if the run cannot reach validation/checkpoint within the short-training cap. The original A02 achieved high memory occupancy around `93GB` but was too slow end-to-end. The correct next engineering target is to remove the I/O bottleneck so a higher batch size can be used without sacrificing training throughput or experiment validity.

## DEC-000035 | 2026-04-25 23:33:22 CST

- Topic: Layered logging and new-window handoff policy
- Requested by: user
- Decision: The project logging system now uses a layered `2+1` policy. `messages.md/jsonl`, `operations.md/jsonl`, and `decisions.md` remain the Raw audit layer and must continue to preserve detailed, append-only traceability. A new short handoff file, `logs/current_state.md`, becomes the default new-window entry and should contain only current effective facts: current goal, phase, latest conclusions, active plan, blockers, next steps, and retrieval keys. Historical details should not be copied into `current_state.md`; when evidence is needed, use Targeted retrieval against the Raw audit logs with stable keys such as experiment IDs, decision IDs, operation IDs, file paths, script names, dates, and run directories. The Milestones layer is intentionally deferred until a phase closes and a stable medium-term summary is worth recording.
- Rationale: The user identified that the previous log system mixed two conflicting jobs: full historical traceability and low-token new-window handoff. Keeping the three core logs as Raw audit preserves evidence, while adding a short current-state card lowers startup cost and prevents each new window from re-reading the full project history. Deferring Milestones avoids unnecessary maintenance while solving the immediate token problem.

## DEC-000036 | 2026-05-05 00:00:00 CST

- Topic: Fourth paper-pack focus for current V1 comparison and idea mining
- Requested by: user
- Decision: A new paper batch `papers/top_venues/batch_04` should be added, still restricted to top-tier main venues only, but focused more tightly on the immediate needs of the current `V1/T01` stage: UHD-relevant dehazing and deblurring comparison papers, high-resolution-friendly restoration designs, train-test mismatch under patch training vs full-image inference, and prior-/texture-guided restoration methods. The venue boundary for this batch is `CVPR`, `ICCV`, and `ECCV`.
- Rationale: The existing paper library is already strong on generic restoration backbones, arbitrary-scale/real-world SR, and diffusion/generative SR. What the current project stage lacks is a dedicated top-venue comparison pack for the exact problems surfaced by our current experiments, especially `UHD_haze`, future `UHD_deblur`, high-frequency modeling, and the patch-train/full-image-test inconsistency that matters for UHD restoration.

## DEC-000037 | 2026-05-05 23:29:28 CST

- Topic: Stage metric target policy from top-venue restoration papers
- Requested by: user
- Decision: The project should use a staged, top-venue-style metric-target policy rather than claiming a fixed literature SOTA threshold that has not been reproduced under the same local protocol. For the current `V1/T01` stage, `PSNR` is the active hard gate because it is already tracked reliably in local runs; `SSIM` should be added as a mandatory companion metric for formal result tables; and `LPIPS` is recommended for real-image tasks such as `UHD_haze` and future `UHD_deblur`. Cross-run interpretation under the same protocol should treat about `+0.3 dB` as a meaningful gain, `+0.5 dB` as clear progress, and `+1.0 dB` or more as a strong improvement. The concrete current targets are: `UHD-LL_down4` next hard goal `official test PSNR >= 26.0 dB`, strong goal `>= 26.5 dB`; `UHD_haze` next hard goal is to complete official test evaluation and stabilize validation/full-val `PSNR >= 20.0 dB`, with a stronger follow-up goal of `official test PSNR >= 20.5 dB` or `+0.5 dB` over the first valid official baseline after pipeline optimization; `UHD_deblur` should first establish a reproducible official baseline in `A03`, then use `+0.5 dB` over that baseline as the next improvement target under the same protocol.
- Rationale: Top-venue restoration papers usually report `PSNR/SSIM`, and real-image restoration often adds perceptual metrics such as `LPIPS`. Our local evidence currently supports a numeric next target for `UHD-LL_down4` and a provisional staged target for `UHD_haze`, but not a defensible absolute SOTA threshold on `UHD_deblur` yet. A staged target policy gives the project a concrete benchmark for the next experiments without overstating what has been verified from the literature.

## DEC-000043 | 2026-05-08 20:20:00 CST

- Topic: Add A_d03 edge-guided detail recovery mechanism
- Requested by: user
- Decision: The next `UHD_deblur` improvement branch is `V1/T01/A_d03`, which adds a default-off high-resolution edge-guided detail refiner and an optional Laplacian high-frequency consistency loss. The refiner computes full-resolution Sobel/Laplacian/local-highpass descriptors from the degraded input, projects them into decoder feature space, fuses them with decoder features and Gaussian attention, and predicts an additional bounded RGB refinement residual. The new branch enables `use_edge_guidance=true`, `edge_guidance_blocks=2`, `edge_guidance_strength=0.25`, and `laplacian_weight=0.02`. To preserve the user's `>=80G` memory floor without OOM, calibration rejects batch `24/23/22/21` and accepts batch `20`, with 20-step smoke `max_memory_reserved_gb=94.1211` and estimated `2575` steps per 60 minutes. Formal preflight passes with `total_params=231528`, `loss_total=0.0986063`, `loss_laplacian=0.0276457`, `val_loss=0.0488943`, and `val_psnr=23.7190`. The run package is ready but not launched yet.
- Rationale: `A_c03` showed that merely increasing batch size satisfies the high-VRAM constraint but does not improve the official baseline. The next branch therefore needs a stronger mechanism aimed at deblurring-relevant high-frequency structure. Keeping the edge refiner and Laplacian loss disabled by default preserves old checkpoint/config compatibility, while enabling them only in `A_d03` gives the project a clear branch to test whether explicit edge/detail recovery improves `UHD_deblur` PSNR over the current `A03 final.pt` baseline.

## DEC-000045 | 2026-05-10 02:05:00 CST

- Topic: Paper-paradigm-driven bottleneck breakthrough strategy
- Requested by: user
- Decision: The project's current bottleneck should be treated as a three-layer stack rather than a single model-capacity problem. Layer 1 is the `UHD_haze` 4K JPEG/DataLoader throughput bottleneck, which must be solved before any meaningful high-VRAM haze-model upgrade. Layer 2 is the `patch train / full-image test` protocol mismatch already exposed by the `A03 -> A_b03 -> A_c03` line, which should be addressed with a `TLC`-style train-inference consistency strategy rather than only changing validation protocol. Layer 3 is the remaining model-expression gap of the current `V1` line on `UHD_haze` and `UHD_deblur`, where the most suitable near-term method upgrades are direction-structured and frequency-selective enhancements inspired by `Stripformer` and `FFTformer`, while keeping the current low-resolution compact modeling plus high-resolution structured reinjection mainline. The project's short-to-mid-term strategy is therefore a dual-track plan: a `V1.5` bridge track that prioritizes `UHD-LL` unified-benchmark closure, `TLC`-style consistency repair on deblurring, direction/frequency enhancement for `UHD_deblur`, and IO-first repair for `UHD_haze`; and a later `V2`-style enhancement track that adds lightweight texture/prior enhancers inspired by `DTPM` and `PTG-RM` as外挂 modules rather than replacing the main restoration backbone. The project should explicitly reject switching the mainline to pure diffusion/generative restoration, all-in-one restoration, or a generic stronger-backbone replacement paper.
- Rationale: Existing local evidence already separates engineering, protocol, and modeling bottlenecks. `A_a02` and the stage report show that `UHD_haze` is blocked first by throughput, not trainability. `A_b03` and `A_c03` show that merely moving to full-image validation and higher VRAM does not fix the official `UHD_deblur` ceiling. The research-direction baseline also explicitly commits the project to a UHD-specific low-resolution compact modeling plus high-resolution structured reinjection story, so the correct use of top-venue papers is to borrow paradigms that reinforce that story rather than replace it.

Official test evaluation, however, does not improve over the accepted current reference `A_a02 final.pt`: `step_002000.pt` reaches `loss_mean=0.1049516647328417`, `PSNR=18.962357169960864`; `step_002500.pt` reaches `loss_mean=0.10202291427236615`, `PSNR=19.396782047049683`; and `final.pt` reaches `loss_mean=0.10090440487513294`, `PSNR=19.439466700482857`. Therefore the best same-protocol official-test result inside `A_b02` is `final.pt`, but it remains about `-0.0049 dB` below `A_a02 final.pt PSNR=19.444372918691123`. The project should record `A_b02` as the first fair cache-backed high-VRAM `UHD_haze` short run and as proof that the IO bottleneck has been solved, but it should **not** replace the current official `UHD_haze` reference result, which remains `A_a02 final.pt`.
- Rationale: `A_b02` answers the engineering question cleanly. The previous `A02` failure was indeed a throughput problem, and the patch-cache route is sufficient to restore a valid high-VRAM short-training envelope. But it also answers a more subtle scientific question: solving the IO bottleneck alone does not automatically lift same-protocol official-test performance. Internal validation gains can be large while official test remains flat, which means the haze line's next bottleneck is no longer data throughput. Future `UHD_haze` work should keep the cache-backed data path, but move on to actual modeling or train-test-consistency changes instead of replaying another pure IO branch.

## DEC-000053 | 2026-05-13 10:44:30 CST

The new mechanism computes an amplitude-weighted compact prior from Gaussian direction, frequency, texture gain, coherence, and spectral decay statistics, upsamples it to high resolution, and injects it into the high-resolution edge-guided detail refiner alongside decoder features, edge descriptors, and Gaussian scalar attention. This branch is chosen because `A_f03` already established that simple budget extension still yields some gain, but only about `+0.1004 dB` over `A_e03`, so the highest-value next question is no longer whether the same branch needs even more steps; it is whether `UHD_deblur` now needs stronger directional/frequency structure. Deployment gate results are complete. Static checks pass, CPU compatibility checks confirm that the accepted `A_f03` path still runs with `directional_prior=None` while the new `A_g03` path activates the prior, and the launch script is syntactically valid. On clean-GPU serial smoke calibration with `PYTORCH_CUDA_ALLOC_CONF=expandable_segments:True`, `batch_size=19` OOMs immediately (`max_memory_reserved_gb=94.103515625`), while `batch_size=18` succeeds for `20` real steps with `max_memory_allocated_gb=92.19077157974243`, `max_memory_reserved_gb=93.013671875`, `avg_total_step_seconds=1.2536016065161675`, and estimated `2871` steps per `60` minutes, so the final launch setting is `batch_size=18`, which still satisfies the user's `>=80G` memory floor. Formal preflight also passes at `batch_size=18`: `train_samples=18`, `val_samples=20`, `batch_input_shape=(18,3,512,512)`, `total_params=246888`, `loss_total=0.25922951102256775`, `val_loss=0.18599891066551208`, `val_psnr=13.65215141359904`. `A_g03` is therefore launch-approved and should be started as the next active `UHD_deblur` run.
- Rationale: The accepted project strategy already ranked stronger direction/frequency structure as a better next move than another near-identical longer-budget replay. `A_g03` is the cleanest way to test that hypothesis without abandoning the current lowres-compact-modeling plus Gaussian-guided highres-reinjection story. The mechanism is additive, default-off, and versioned; it preserves compatibility with prior branches while making the high-resolution detail path more aware of blur direction and frequency structure. Because the activation footprint is slightly larger than `A_f03`, the deployment setting must be recalibrated rather than copied mechanically; the measured `batch_size=18` envelope provides a stable high-VRAM package that remains within the user's gate.

## DEC-000064 | 2026-05-14 15:15:00 CST

- Topic: Elevate lightweight-priority and 90%-of-strong-reference goal to a top-level project constraint
- Requested by: user
- Decision: The project now adopts a new top-level optimization rule: **lightweight priority is mandatory**, because the core identity of this line is that `2D Gaussian` should help make UHD restoration lighter rather than merely acting as a decorative auxiliary module. Therefore, future model evolution must preserve the lightweight `lowres compact modeling + highres structured reinjection` story, and should not trade away the method’s lightweight nature by drifting into obviously heavier backbone-dominated solutions, even if such changes might improve absolute metrics. At the same time, the short-to-mid-term performance goal is redefined from “try to chase full SOTA” to “reach at least 90% of the current strong public reference under the same official benchmark protocol.” For internal project management, this 90% rule is operationally defined as `PSNR >= 0.9 × current strong public reference PSNR` on the same benchmark and official evaluation protocol, while formal scientific reporting should still prioritize absolute PSNR/SSIM/LPIPS and explicit dB gaps. Under the current unified benchmark table, the working 90% PSNR thresholds are: `UHD-LL >= 24.4431 dB` (from `27.159`), `UHD-Haze >= 20.4525 dB` (from `22.725`), and `UHD-Blur >= 26.5743 dB` (from `29.527`). The current local status is that `UHD_deblur` is below this threshold by about `0.7237 dB` (`A_i03 = 25.8506`), `UHD_haze` is below by about `1.0081 dB` (`A_a02 = 19.4444`), and `UHD-LL` still lacks a strict unified-benchmark official result because the existing positive result is on `UHD-LL_down4`. This rule should govern future experiment design, branch approval, and template evolution. The detailed explanation is recorded in `/root/autodl-tmp/2DGauss/docs/lightweight_priority_and_90pct_goal_20260514.md`.
- Rationale: The user explicitly clarified that lightweightness is not optional but central to the project’s value, and also set a pragmatic performance expectation: not necessarily matching the very best SOTA, but reaching at least 90% of strong public results. Formalizing this as a decision prevents future turns from drifting into “heavier model for a few more dB” logic and gives the project a stable, auditable success criterion that is better aligned with its intended scientific contribution.

Relative to the frozen previous baseline `A_i03 final.pt PSNR=25.85061787687282`, `A_k03 final.pt` improves by about `+0.3493 dB`; relative to `A_g03 final.pt`, the gain is about `+0.8199 dB`. The project should therefore record `A_k03 final.pt` as the new official `UHD_deblur` baseline. At the same time, under the newly adopted lightweight `90%` rule for `UHD-Blur`, the branch still remains below the current threshold `26.5743 dB` by about `0.3743 dB`.

This makes `A_k03` strong enough to replace `A_i03` as the local official baseline, but the result also sharpens the next-stage interpretation: even a clean `+0.3493 dB` from longer training is still not enough to cross the lightweight `90%` target, so future gains should not rely only on more budget or near-isomorphic replay.

These large gaps should **not** be interpreted as a failure of same-benchmark progress on `UHD_haze` or `UHD_deblur`; rather, they confirm a more specific fact already hinted by the paper itself: the current line is specialized to UHD-native distributions and transfers poorly to general-resolution dehazing/deblurring benchmarks without corresponding task-specific training. ## DEC-000071 | 2026-05-14 19:58:21 CST

- Topic: Choose Gaussian-guided decoder modulation as the next lightweight architectural upgrade after `A_k03/M03`
- Requested by: user
- Decision: The next post-`A_k03/M03` architectural upgrade on the `UHD_deblur` mainline should be a **default-off, lightweight Gaussian-guided decoder modulation** mechanism rather than a wider/heavier backbone replacement or another near-identical long-training replay. Concretely, the accepted `A_k03` line already uses Gaussian information through rendered attention, low-rank affine modulation, directional-frequency prior, and edge-guided refinement. The newly selected upgrade extends that same Gaussian-first story one step deeper into the high-resolution reconstruction path: a compact prior stack derived from the rendered attention map, local attention contrast, and optional directional prior is encoded by a small depthwise residual adapter and used to predict bounded feature-wise scale/bias that modulate the decoder feature before the final RGB prediction. This mechanism must remain disabled by default so that old configs and frozen milestone checkpoints stay behaviorally unchanged; only the new design-only branch config `A_l03` enables it explicitly. The project should treat this as the current preferred lightweight template-upgrade direction because it increases the direct influence of Gaussian priors on high-resolution reconstruction without materially rewriting the backbone or diluting the `2D Gaussian` identity into a generic heavier restorer.
- Rationale: The user asked to continue improving absolute performance but explicitly keep the mainline lightweight and Gaussian-centered. `A_k03` already showed that "more training time" is useful yet insufficient on its own, while the current remaining gap to the lightweight `90%` gate is small enough that a focused structural upgrade is more informative than another purely budget-based replay. Decoder-stage Gaussian-guided modulation is the most aligned next step because it preserves the accepted `A_k03` training recipe, reuses already validated Gaussian-derived priors, and adds only a small number of parameters compared with increasing width/depth globally.

- Decision: The project should attribute the success of `A_y03/M04` to a specific lightweight mechanism combination rather than to generic longer training or heavier modeling. The current strongest explanation is: (1) Gaussian prior placement was improved by moving its main effect from terminal RGB residual control to decoder feature-space refinement; (2) edge-weighted reconstruction was kept but rebalanced down to the lighter `0.02` regime, avoiding the over-regularization signal seen around the earlier combined short probes; and (3) the medium-budget five-hour window then had enough room to convert that better structural bias into official-test gain. The surrounding evidence chain is now coherent: `A_q03` ruled out direct Gaussian-guided terminal residual control as the mainline upgrade path; `A_r03` and `A_s03` showed that feature-refinement-only or supervision-only changes are individually too weak; `A_t03` showed the combination direction is real but still too tight; `A_u03` identified lighter edge supervision as the better local rebalance; `A_x03` first scaled that line into a positive medium-budget result; and `A_y03` confirmed it under a fresh seed. The supplemental post-hoc panel further sharpens the interpretation: `step_021000.pt` is the best internal-validation periodic checkpoint, but `final.pt` still wins on official test by about `+0.0521 dB`, so the accepted gain is not merely a validation-slice artifact. Future mainline work should therefore use `M04/A_y03` as the new comparison anchor and preserve the “Gaussian as structured feature-space guidance plus light auxiliary edge pressure” balance, rather than reverting to stronger terminal RGB control or indiscriminately increasing supervision strength.
- Rationale: The user did not only ask for milestone isolation; they also asked for a clear post-hoc explanation of why this experiment worked. Recording the success mechanism as a standalone decision prevents future turns from narrating `A_y03` as a black-box lucky run or from drifting back toward already weakened design directions. It also gives the project a sharper technical basis for the next round of lightweight absolute-performance improvements.

## DEC-000104 | 2026-05-18 00:55:00 CST

- Topic: Use targeted top-venue literature to lock the next lightweight deblur directions onto confidence-normalized control and shared-prior coupling
- Requested by: user
- Decision: After targeted literature review around uncertainty-aware restoration, prompt/degradation-aware modulation, adaptive routing, and UHD-efficient restoration, the project should now explicitly prioritize `A_ac03` and `A_ad03` as the next two lightweight mainline directions, and de-prioritize heavier prompt/MoE/generative branches for the immediate short-probe stage. The literature signal is coherent. `UMRL` (`CVPR 2019`) and `Uncertainty-Aware Unsupervised Image Deblurring with Deep Residual Prior` (`CVPR 2023`) both reinforce that restoration should modulate correction strength according to confidence or uncertainty rather than treating all regions as equally trustworthy. `AirNet` (`CVPR 2022`), `PromptIR` (`NeurIPS 2023`), and `PromptRestorer` (`NeurIPS 2023`) reinforce that the real value of conditional branches is to provide a shared control interface for the restoration backbone rather than to append more raw cues. `Attention-Based Adaptive Selection of Operations for Image Restoration` (`CVPR 2019`), `Ingredient-Oriented Multi-Degradation Learning` (`CVPR 2023`), `Degradation-Aware Feature Perturbation for All-in-One Image Restoration` (`CVPR 2025`), and `Complexity Experts are Task-Discriminative Learners for Any Image Restoration` (`CVPR 2025`) further support the view that selective routing or perturbation is more valuable than direct cue accumulation. Finally, `UHD-processer` (`CVPR 2025`) shows that in UHD settings the hard part concentrates in higher-frequency recovery, so any control mechanism should prefer hard-region / high-frequency selectivity over uniform global strengthening. Therefore the immediate lightweight roadmap should be: first, add a confidence-normalized Gaussian refinement path where edge/high-frequency information calibrates modulation strength instead of entering direct fusion; second, explore a shared prior-control stem consumed by both decoder guidance and feature refinement; and only after those are tested consider heavier teacher-prior, MoE, or generative-prior branches.
- Rationale: The project already has strong local evidence that “one more cue” is not the right frame. This literature review does not replace the experimental evidence; it sharpens it and reduces the risk of wasting short-budget probes on fashionable but mismatched directions. Recording the decision now keeps future experiment design aligned with the lightweight Gaussian-first identity while still being grounded in current top-venue trends.

## DEC-000110 | 2026-05-18 22:35:25 CST

All three branches must keep `M04/A_y03` frozen-isolated, stay inside the short-tier budget, and preserve the current dataset and evaluation protocol. The key policy point is that these branches are diagnostic probes, not a new cue stack: each one changes a different control function in the Gaussian-decoder surface, so their outcomes can tell us whether the remaining bottleneck is closed-loop regulation, positive demand formation, or module role separation.
- Rationale: The user explicitly asked for several short probes that dig into the core bottleneck and avoid standing still. The strongest evidence so far says the project already has enough signals; what it lacks is a selective, coordinated control mechanism. Splitting the next work into three orthogonal probes maximizes diagnostic value while keeping the runtime envelope and milestone isolation intact.

## DEC-000114 | 2026-05-19 12:04:32 CST

- Topic: Use top-venue literature to reframe the next bottleneck move as structured control rather than another single gate
- Requested by: user
- Decision: After reviewing representative top-venue restoration papers, the next useful design family should move from single multiplicative gating toward structured multi-branch control. `MPRNet` (`CVPR 2021`) and `Uformer` / `Restormer` (`CVPR 2022`) emphasize staged or hierarchical restoration with explicit information exchange; `AirNet` (`CVPR 2022`) and `PromptIR` / `PromptRestorer` (`NeurIPS 2023`) show that prompt or degradation priors are most effective when routed through a dedicated control branch; `Multimodal Prompt Perceiver` (`CVPR 2024`), `DFPIR` (`CVPR 2025`), `Complexity Experts` (`CVPR 2025`), `UHD-Processor` (`CVPR 2025`), and `UniRestore` (`CVPR 2025`) reinforce the same pattern through dual-branch prompting, routing/perturbation, frequency-progressive learning, and task adapters. The shared lesson for our project is that the remaining `A_ai03`-type bottleneck should be treated as a cross-module closed-loop coordination problem, not as a missing single cue. Therefore the next short probe family should be designed around staged/routed/shared-control scheduling that jointly coordinates decoder detail, edge-role separation, and shared prior, while keeping `M04/A_y03` frozen and not replaying `A_ai03` unchanged.
- Rationale: The user explicitly asked to look at top-tier papers for ideas. The evidence across the selected papers is coherent enough to move the project away from one more scalar gate and toward a structured control surface.

## DEC-000118 | 2026-05-19 16:55:51 CST

The active config is `experiments/V1/runs/A_ap03/configs/a_ap03_short_uhddeblur_demand_residual_soft_router_probe.toml` with `gaussian_control_router_role_delta_strength = 0.42`, `gaussian_control_router_role_competition_strength = 0.30`, and `gaussian_control_router_role_competition_floor = 0.45`. The initial `6x696` preflight OOMed after reaching the late forward path, so the demand branch was made lightweight and geometry was retuned. `6x688` formal preflight passed with `total_params=348700` and `val_psnr=17.34742212473538`, but true smoke exceeded the reserved-memory window at `max_memory_reserved_gb=93.79296875`. The accepted launch geometry is therefore `batch_size=6`, `train_context_size=680`, `train_supervision_crop_size=512`; at this geometry formal preflight passed with `batch_input_shape=(6, 3, 680, 680)`, `total_params=348700`, and `val_psnr=17.340130889173448`, and true smoke passed with `max_memory_reserved_gb=91.56640625`, `avg_total_step_seconds=2.008628693098823`, and `within_target_reserved_memory_window=true`. `A_ap03` has been launched as tmux session `2dgauss_a_ap03_uhddeblur_demand_residual_soft_router_probe`, with startup log `experiments/V1/runs/A_ap03/logs/train_20260519_165513.log`; startup metrics are writing through at least `step=50`, with `loss_total=0.032103922218084335`, no NaN/OOM, and GPU utilization at `100%`.
- Rationale: The user explicitly asked to continue adjusting and break the bottleneck. The newest evidence says the bottleneck is not router trainability, but unstable cross-split role selectivity caused by hard role competition. `A_ap03` directly attacks that root cause by preserving role cooperation early, constraining role deltas to residual specialization, and allowing competition only later and only under learned demand.

## DEC-000121 | 2026-05-19 21:47:47 CST

- Topic: Reframe the post-report mainline around explicit frequency splitting, Gaussian control-canvas semantics, Gaussian-guided feedback, and real-effect evaluation
- Requested by: user
- Decision: The advisor feedback after the current report should become the next mainline policy, not just a presentation note. The project should pivot from simply continuing router variants toward a deeper and more explainable Gaussian-guided restoration mechanism. The next work must explicitly address four points. First, the branch entry must explain and preferably implement how low-frequency/structure information and high-frequency/detail information are separated; the current `pixel_unshuffle + HR high-pass` design is a useful engineering baseline but is not yet a fully explicit, visualizable frequency split. Second, every module must be technically accountable: current `A_y03` uses an HR `3x3 Conv2d + depthwise residual` detail branch, LR `pixel_unshuffle(scale=4) + 1x1 projection + residual depthwise blocks`, Sobel/Laplacian texture-frequency descriptors, a 10-parameter Gaussian renderer, low-rank affine modulation, Gaussian decoder guidance, feature-space refinement, and edge residual refinement. Third, the Gaussian heatmap should be described as an interpretable control canvas carrying restoration demand, position, anisotropic scale, direction, frequency tendency, and confidence, not as a direct RGB texture generator or generic attention map; the core paper innovation should be Gaussian-guided feedback/reinjection from the LR structure canvas back into HR detail and decoder states. Fourth, the dehazing samples must be treated as a real-effect failure/diagnostic for the deblur-trained checkpoint: PSNR/SSIM alone are insufficient, and future evaluation should include perceptual/visual quality, color/contrast, artifacts, and haze-specific behavior if dehazing is pursued. The proposed next experiment/design family should therefore prioritize explicit frequency split probes, Gaussian control-canvas auxiliary visualization/reconstruction, closed-loop Gaussian feedback controllers, and separate haze qualitative diagnostics. Do not launch `A_an03/A_ao03` as inertia, and do not treat another router tweak as the only mainline.

The main risk is replacing PSNR/SSIM overdominance with proxy-loss overdominance, causing over-sharpening, halo/ringing, over-enhancement, color drift, dark-channel artifacts, or low-light noise amplification. The new guardrails and audit document make these failure modes explicit and put technical limits around future configurations.

- Rationale: `A_az03` proved that structured control demand exists and can be isolated safely, but it still lost to `A_aw03`, which means the bottleneck is no longer heatmap activation or control-path interpretability. The most direct next question is whether that control signal simply lacks a sufficiently strong entry point into the actual high-frequency/detail restoration path. Routing `control_attention_map` into the explicit detail-injection branch is the narrowest, cleanest, and safest test of demand-to-detail coupling because it changes one downstream entry point while leaving the protected main backbone and the accepted non-saturated feedback regime unchanged.

## DEC-000139 | 2026-05-21 11:56:39 CST

- Topic: Treat `A_ba03` as a likely negative coupling diagnostic, and pivot the next breakthrough attempt to decoupled selectivity and correction strength
- Requested by: user
- Decision: `A_ba03` should be treated as a likely negative diagnostic on the post-`A_az03` bottleneck rather than a reason to further strengthen direct control-demand detail injection. The next breakthrough attempt should not keep increasing the same direct coupling path. Instead, the project should pivot to a more precise mechanism: decouple "where to correct" from "how much to correct", and stop the high-detail restoration branch from backpropagating in a way that flattens the structured control signal. Concretely, the next short probe should keep the proven protection recipe from `A_az03/A_aw03` intact, but replace `A_ba03`'s direct control-attention-driven high-detail injection with a two-factor correction design: a structure-preserving control selectivity map, preferably gradient-isolated or otherwise protected from being collapsed by the restoration loss, and a separate residual/detail amplitude path predicted from restoration features. The intended effect is that the control branch still answers "where correction is needed", while the restoration branch answers "how strong the correction should be", instead of forcing one signal to serve both roles.
- Rationale: The `A_ba03` evidence already narrows the failure mode. Compared with `A_az03`, it increases `guided_high_detail std≈0.14300` and `restoration_residual std≈0.04401`, so the model is indeed injecting more high-frequency/residual energy. But the structured control pathway is compressed at the same time: `attention_bridge_map std≈0.03018` versus `≈0.06995`, `control_attention_map std≈0.00905` versus `≈0.02098`, and `control_canvas std≈0.02446` versus `≈0.05496`, while panel restoration quality falls sharply (`restored_psnr≈22.89967` versus `≈23.89168`, `restored_ssim≈0.63023` versus `≈0.72655`). This shows the current bottleneck is not simply insufficient correction strength. It is the conflict between stronger correction and preserved structured selectivity. A better next probe is therefore not "more direct control-demand injection", but "protect selectivity, then modulate amplitude separately."

## DEC-000164 | 2026-06-08 20:14:12 CST

- Topic: Old-mainline model bottleneck after returning from deshaking branch
- Requested by: user
- Decision: The current old-mainline model bottleneck is `high-resolution detail expression and official full-image generalization under the lightweight Gaussian-guided architecture`, not dataset organization, training-entry viability, or a basic GPU/runtime failure. `M04/A_y03` should remain the strongest confirmed `UHD_deblur` anchor, and its success mechanism should be interpreted narrowly: Gaussian works best here as structured feature-space guidance plus light edge-weighted pressure, not as direct terminal RGB residual control. The next model work should therefore avoid blind backbone enlargement or repeated near-identical long runs, and instead focus on narrow variables that strengthen direction/frequency selection, patch-train/full-image-test consistency, and task-specific priors for dehazing/deraining/low-light. The `UHD_haze` evidence should be read similarly: after `A_b02` fixed the patch-cache/IO bottleneck but failed to beat `A_a02` on official test, the remaining haze bottleneck is now modeling/protocol/task-prior, not pure IO.
- Rationale: The historical deblur sequence isolates the model bottleneck well. `A_p03` showed stable medium-budget training but stayed below `A_k03`; `A_q03` proved direct Gaussian terminal RGB residual control is a negative path; `A_s03` showed loss-only edge pressure is insufficient; `A_r03` was near-neutral; and the `A_t03 -> A_u03 -> A_x03 -> A_y03` chain confirmed that feature-space Gaussian refinement plus lighter edge pressure is the useful direction. Even so, `A_y03=26.272695829299355 dB` remains about `0.3016 dB` below the project `UHD-Blur` 90% target `26.5743 dB`, so the main gap is not solved. This decision keeps future work anchored to the confirmed mechanism while preventing drift back to already falsified terminal-control, loss-only, pure-IO, or simply-heavier-backbone explanations.

## DEC-000168 | 2026-06-10 11:07:55 CST

- Topic: Recent-work review and anti-rabbit-hole correction
- Requested by: user
- Decision: The recent work should be read as useful engineering recovery and state alignment, not as a scientific breakthrough phase. The project has made real progress by returning from the paused deshake side branch to the old UHD restoration task, unpacking and auditing the current Baidu dataset copy, preparing T01 training inventory, and proving with `A_4kd02` that low-footprint GPU0/3-only training can run safely on the shared server without displacing other users. However, the active research bottleneck has not moved: the project is still blocked by official/full-protocol comparability and by the old-mainline mechanism gap in selective, fidelity-protected Gaussian-to-decoder control. Continuing to run more `4KDehaze` fast-probe variants under the same `512/100/100` subset would become a rabbit hole unless each run tests a sharply new mechanism or prepares a path to an official/full split benchmark. The next phase should therefore stop treating fast-probe PSNR as progress by itself, restore or define comparable evaluation protocols first, and focus mechanism work on confidence/demand-aware, fidelity-protected, shared Gaussian control rather than adding more local cues or repeating low-footprint training variants.
- Rationale: `A_4kd02` solved an operational question: how to train safely under GPU0/3 shared-server pressure. It did not solve the paper question. Historical evidence from `M04/A_y03`, `A_aa03/A_ab03`, `A_aq03/A_ar03/A_as03`, and the documented bottleneck analyses already shows that the hard problem is not another cue, more batch, or another near-identical probe; it is selective coordination between Gaussian priors and the high-resolution decoder, plus patch/full-image protocol consistency and task-specific priors. Important correction: the project does have reloadable milestone/reference weights and accepted metrics on this server, including `M04_A_y03_uhddeblur_official_baseline` and `A_a02`; the comparability issue is not lack of anchors. The remaining protocol gap is that current-server raw `UHD_deblur`/`UHD_haze` directories are not present in the Baidu dataset root, while the `UHD_haze` patch cache does exist. Therefore old-anchor results remain valid frozen baselines, but new same-protocol full/raw re-evaluation or training must either restore the missing raw official data paths, use the existing haze cache for its exact patch protocol, or define an explicit current-server official protocol. This decision is a strategy correction: keep the safe GPU discipline from `A_4kd02`, but do not let shared-GPU convenience or fast-probe metrics define the research direction.

## DEC-000169 | 2026-06-10 11:16:19 CST

- Topic: Bottleneck-solving route without strict historical re-evaluation gate
- Requested by: user
- Decision: The next bottleneck-solving phase should not require strict re-running of historical official benchmarks before any progress. Existing milestone/reference weights and accepted metrics are sufficient as orientation anchors. The project should move directly to a mechanism-first route: build a demand/confidence-aware, fidelity-protected shared Gaussian control interface that decides where to repair, where to preserve, and how strongly to route correction into the high-resolution decoder. The first useful family should be a narrow probe rather than a broad architecture rewrite: keep the `M04/A_y03` lesson of Gaussian feature-space guidance plus light edge pressure, replace generic structure/edge cue accumulation with a restoration-demand map derived from input-vs-target residual structure, use bounded additive feature correction instead of broad multiplicative modulation, add gate-budget/confidence normalization to prevent all-image overcorrection, and let decoder guidance / feature refinement / detail correction consume one shared control surface rather than independent weak gates.
- Rationale: The user correctly noted that the project already has milestone weights, so strict historical re-evaluation would be unnecessary process friction. The real unresolved issue is mechanistic: previous attempts showed that structure/control maps can be made more visible, but they do not automatically improve official fidelity because they are not aligned to restoration demand and lack identity protection. This route attacks that root cause directly. It also gives each new fast or low-footprint run a falsifiable purpose: success is not merely better fast-probe PSNR, but evidence that the learned control is selective, avoids damaging easy/clean regions, improves task-specific diagnostics, and moves toward the frozen anchor behavior without reverting to previously rejected direct RGB terminal residual control or raw cue injection.

The task-specific diagnostics remain mixed: dark-channel, luminance, saturation, and SSIM improve, while edge/high-frequency/local-contrast/chroma proxies worsen. - Rationale: A19 separates "A18 overfit the first 100-sample fast-probe block" from "A08/A15 have stable sample-adaptive complementarity." The fixed A18 threshold still gains `+0.028283712021403673 dB` over the stronger single checkpoint on non-overlapping tail test samples, and retuning on tail validation changes the threshold only slightly while adding just about `+0.00115 dB`. The binary oracle on tail test remains higher at `15.928149885150932`, so there is still headroom. The next useful step is to implement a lightweight learned or model-internal router that predicts the A08/A15 choice or correction regime without two full checkpoint forwards, then validate that design on larger/current split subsets before any official/full claim.

## DEC-000189 | 2026-06-13 13:45:17 CST

- Topic: Reframe the bottleneck as conditional specialization not yet represented by one checkpoint
- Requested by: user
- Decision: The project should now treat the lack of final breakthrough as a representation/conversion problem, not as absence of signal. The validated signal is A08/A15 conditional complementarity: the fixed A18 `input_std` router beats either single checkpoint across A18/A19/A21 blocks. The reason the project has not broken through is that the current single-checkpoint attempts use global/shared-path pressure and cannot store both A08-like high-`input_std` preservation and A15-like low-`input_std` gains. Future A24-style work should therefore move from weak scalar loss sweeps to structural single-model routing: hard route labels, balanced high/low `input_std` sampling, route-conditioned corrections, dual-head/internal gating, A08 high-`input_std` branch protection, and residual/correction distillation.
- Rationale: A23 is the decisive negative example. It proves default-off router-distillation infrastructure works, but weak whole-output distillation from an A15-initialized student stays A15-like (`17.78064128762362` PSNR) and does not approach the A18 two-checkpoint router (`17.84693437641316`). A17 similarly showed that teacher consistency applies the right idea too globally and harms A15 gains. A22 showed free richer-feature learned routers overfit and do not beat the fixed rule aggregate. Together these results imply the next breakthrough must explicitly encode the route behavior inside the model, then validate cross-block before candidate promotion.

A26 shows hard crop-aware 50/50 route sampling technically balances route labels but hurts validation (`18.236443073291603` early best vs A24 `18.349088096641417`), so the missing ingredient is not simply exposure. ## DEC-000213 | 2026-06-17 18:31:46 CST

- Topic: Stop treating A37/A47-style route-gate patches as the main breakthrough path
- Requested by: user
- Decision: The project should not continue the current V1/T01 line as an open-ended sequence of small gate, sampler, scalar-loss, or late-adapter patches. A48 selective positive-up with hard negatives may still be allowed as a bounded falsification probe, but the main research direction should pivot to a new B-series architecture that adds route-specific internal capacity and a richer learned route boundary. The useful core should be preserved: A36 as full-capacity upper bound, A37 as compressed/shared aggregate milestone, A40 as aggregate-only trade-off evidence, and A46/A47 as direct evidence of zero-sum route gating.
- Rationale: A39-A47 now show a repeated Pareto trade-off rather than cumulative progress. A40 improves aggregate test PSNR to `17.900562951956445` and low/A15 gaps to `+0.18311149168725271` / `+0.3023396530841825`, but worsens high/A08 to `-0.1180115677202543` / `-0.23833660113789967` compared with A37. A46 final does the reverse: high/A08 improve to `-0.10929431364232904` / `-0.1659231102631667`, but aggregate/low/A15 collapse to `17.87790077389511`, `+0.1367771104331444`, and `+0.1986870903736065`. A47 protects low/A15 and returns to A37-like aggregate, but suppresses high/A08 again. This is a clear "拆东墙补西墙" pattern caused by the current architecture: a compact shared backbone with mostly late route heads and tiny gates lacks enough route-specific internal capacity to represent A08-like high-fidelity preservation and A15-like low-route restoration simultaneously. The next serious architecture should split or adapt capacity inside the model, not only at the output gate: slim dual-expert decoder, multi-depth conditional adapters/LoRA experts, learned content-aware boundary router using input_std as prior rather than truth, and route-specific Gaussian control channels.

## DEC-000214 | 2026-06-17 18:45 CST

- Topic: Mechanism feasibility audit and stop-blind-training rule
- Requested by: user
- Decision: No new training should be launched from the current thread until a written experiment card passes the mechanism gate. The current A37/A47 route-patch line is paused as an open-ended training line. Mechanisms are classified as follows. Keep as foundations: A08/A15 complementarity, `input_std` prior, A36 full route-specific capacity upper bound, A37 native-teacher compressed/shared milestone, and A18/A36/A37 alignment diagnostics. Keep only as tools: hard-case cache/sampler, bounded residual gate, trust-anchor plumbing, checkpoint surgery, and late route residual diagnostics. Stop as mainline: output/post adapters, terminal/head-only tuning, shared-control-only tuning, high-sampler-only tuning, scalar oracle weighting, teacher L1-only promotion, selected-output anchors without internal capacity, gate-only hard-case correction, and aggregate PSNR-only promotion.
- Rationale: The experiment data now separate signal from bottleneck. A18 (`17.84693437581442`) and A36 (`17.8470899392002`, high gap `-0.00019952460737376242`, low gap `+0.00042343678412253124`) prove the route signal and full route-specific capacity work. A37 (`17.889658453438816`) proves partial compression can beat the router aggregate line. But A40, A46, and A47 show the current shared compact architecture plus late route heads/gates is zero-sum: A40 improves aggregate/low/A15 while worsening high/A08; A46 improves high/A08 while collapsing aggregate/low/A15; A47 restores low/A15 while giving back high/A08. Therefore more blind training is likely to repeat the same trade-off. The next serious work must be B-series route-specific internal capacity with a learned/content-aware boundary, and every run must define expected movement, allowed regression, checkpoint selection, and stop condition before training.

Therefore the new architecture must keep shared Gaussian structure while adding explicit route-specific internal capacity. A full dual backbone is valid but not a compact paper contribution; a learned router alone is insufficient; a bigger shared backbone risks repeating A39. A route-specific Gaussian adapter decoder offers the best compromise: it preserves the project's Gaussian-control identity while moving capacity to the internal feature path where A35/A38/A46 showed late patches are too weak.

The bottleneck is not the Gaussian prior itself; it is the lack of route-specific interpretation of that prior for high-preserve/A08 and low-dehaze/A15 regimes. Therefore the next breakthrough attempt should rebuild the surrounding routing/control mechanisms around Gaussian rather than swapping Gaussian out or continuing late route patches.

The issue is therefore no longer ordinary validation acceptance, missing transfer metrics, projection plumbing, GPU, teacher plumbing, or scalar threshold choice. The next G-RSCN probe should escalate structurally to split Gaussian-conditioned high-preserve and low-dehaze route-control capacity, while retaining the A18 transfer-aware guard and A37-aligned promotion gates. Do not lengthen G12 or rerun it with only scalar/threshold tweaks.
